amendment 3 §2; targets: prereg §5.1 + amendment 4 §1; readings, labels and headline: amendment 5 §3–§4). It decides
the open questions at the end of each `register_work/<key>.md` draft. **The drafts are incorporated by reference** at
the sha256 of §8: every per-claim field a draft gives (quote, location, published value, map and file hashes, set and
its source, T, direction, inference level, analysis class, reproduction target, dependencies, material list) is part of
this register unless a decision below changes it; the drafts' "proposed" alternatives not adopted here are superseded.
Every registered cell is restated with its final fields in `claims_register.tsv` (one row per cell; §4). Where this
file, the TSV and a draft disagree, this file governs, then the TSV.

## 1. Conventions

### 1.1 Cells: claims, descriptor cells, published-null cells
- A **claim** is (map y, set S or component, statistic T, direction), per prereg §4 / §4.7 and amendment 4 §2.
- **Granularity rule G.** A cell is a registered **claim** iff the paper's text (abstract, results or discussion) states
  it as a finding, or a main-text figure or table that the text cites for that finding marks it significant. A set is
  "named" by the text only if the name the text (or the cited figure or table) prints reaches a term of the pinned release
  by the **name rule** below (database prefixes such as GOBP_ removed when the text is compared); thematic summaries do not
  name sets. A statement of a range of stages or regions that does not name a set bin ("from early to mid-fetal onward")
  names no cell. A cross-product of
  regions and stages that the text lists together is read as asserting every listed combination (Benkarim, Park).
  Every other cell of the paper's factorial is a **descriptor cell**: computed with the same machinery, reported in full,
  never a claim, a survival, or part of m. Descriptor cells are registered where the cell can be enumerated with a
  defined set; a collective statement of non-enrichment without a defined set (e.g. Pagani's "no enrichment for bipolar
  disorder, psoriasis, …") is recorded in the TSV notes, not registered. A **family** (several cells sharing one
  undefined set, e.g. RG-03's 11 KEGG pathways; BE-11, BE-12) is one registered unit and counts once wherever units are
  counted; its cell count is fixed when its defining table is read (under its gate), reported, and never changes m or
  the headline.
- **Name rule (v6, R1; one rule for every GO, KEGG, MSigDB, Reactome and figure-label mapping in this register).** The
  name as the paper prints it (as the register or its draft transcribes it) maps to a term of the pinned release by the
  first of: **(a)** the exact name in the pinned release; else **(b)** a synonym of any scope (EXACT, BROAD, NARROW,
  RELATED), or a former name, that identifies exactly one live term in the pinned release — labelled "S matched by
  <scope> synonym"; else **(c)** case, UK / US spelling or singular / plural normalisation that identifies exactly one term
  (normalised names first, then normalised synonyms of any scope) — labelled with the normalisations needed; else **"S
  undefined"**. Surrounding and repeated white space is ignored; a tier that matches two or more live terms identifies
  none and the next tier is tried; the pinned files record former names only as synonyms (GO keeps a renamed term's
  former name as a synonym; KEGG, MSigDB, WebGestalt and Enrichr libraries serve no synonyms). The normalisations, word
  lists and suffix rules are pinned in `src_register/name_match.py`; every mapped row carries its tier and label in the
  TSV (`set_rule_labels`, `set_status`) and in `register_sets/NAME_MATCHES.json`, per cell, since cells sharing a set may
  reach it by different tiers. **Declared:** the rule was fixed by the orchestrator under the operator's standing
  delegation after the v5 S-build outcome was seen; it replaces a reading applied unevenly (v5 §1.11 item 2: GO names
  needed an exact name or an EXACT synonym, case-insensitive, while KEGG names were matched case-insensitively and after a
  UK → US substitution that labelled the shared set, not the row).
- **Published-null cells** (the paper reports absence or non-significance as its finding: Giacomel's ASD cells; Liu T3's
  "trend"): run like claims, two-sided, outside every survival count; read "detectable by the verdict (the published
  analysis reported none)" or "not detectable at this power (as published)", each with amendment 5's calibration label
  for its set size and class (e.g. "calibration extrapolated (set size)" for m < 25; single-class subcortical cells);
  a resolution-limited cell is reported as such.
- **Non-ASD registered outcomes** (Giacomel's MDD cells, prereg §8; Moreau's CNV class, amendment 4 §2) are reported
  apart from every ASD count.
- **Not ASD claims:** claims a target paper makes about neurotypical participants only (Berto BE-02 … BE-05) are
  registered, reported, and kept out of the ASD headline.

### 1.2 The headline (as frozen; this register only pins its population)
The headline is amendment 5 §3's **frozen-11 survival fraction over claims** ("the fraction counts every survival").
From the frozen texts:
- **Population (denominator):** the registered claims of the frozen-11 targets (prereg §5.1 rows), excluding only what a
  frozen text excludes — descriptor cells, published-null cells, non-ASD outcomes and not-ASD claims (§1.1; they are not
  the papers' positive ASD claims), claims **not testable** in prereg §4's mechanical sense (< 5 genes in the universe,
  counted as columns of the class matrix, §1.6 R5;
  §4.6 "testable … claims"), full-verdict claims flagged **not reproduced** by the §1.5 check (prereg §4.1 "counted in a
  separate column"; §4.6 "reproduced claims"), and **deferred** claims (amendment 4 §0: listed apart) — and
  **including** as non-survivals the claims that are not auditable (prereg §5.1), component-level or "no calibrated test"
  (amendment 5 §3), and **"not testable as stated"** (§1.4; the register's own category, distinct from prereg §4's
  mechanical "not testable", so no frozen text excludes it; kept in, conservatively). A family counts once.
- **Numerator:** claims of the population that survive (status ok; Level 0, site and species leg each < .025; amendment 3
  §2).
- **Beside it, in the same sentence** (amendment 5 §3): the curves (C-sp .970 at a = .9; C-gene .585–.666 at a = .9;
  C-align .018–.074 / .060–.196 / .106–.352 at a = .3 / .5 / .7); gates 1–11 "failed as gates under amendment 3"; the
  number of survivals reading "calibrated evidence" and per scope label; the composition of the denominator by status —
  tested, not auditable, not testable as stated, component-level (and how many pass at component level), no calibrated
  test, and — listed apart — deferred (per gate), each claim counted once by §1.8's precedence order (v6, R8), with the
  cross-tab of component-level-in-form × not auditable beside it (so every claim of component form is visible, whichever
  category counts it) and the claims whose S is conditional on a gated carrier (§1.6, R2) marked provisional — with the
  **tested-only fraction** (survivals / full-verdict claims that received a verdict; component-level claims excluded,
  since they cannot survive) stated alongside, so the reader sees how much of the fraction measures auditability. A low
  fraction reads "few published claims are detectable by a calibrated test at this power", never "few are true".
- **Registered descriptors (never headline):** per-row view — one focal claim per frozen-11 row: the first cell in text
  order that states the row's principal positive ASD finding and has a full-verdict form (set-vs-map, decoded-list or PLS
  enrichment), whatever its material status; a row whose principal finding has no such cell has none (§4 names each with
  its text location); per-target "any registered claim survives" (not multiplicity-controlled); the added nine and their union with
  the frozen 11 (amendment 4 §0.1); the verdict table stratified by inference level; BH q-values (amendment 3 §2).
- **Interim until every gate has opened and its claims have run** (amendment 4 §0: the final headline includes deferred
  claims once they have run). While any gate is closed, the interim fraction is reported with the deferred claims listed
  apart and counted neither way, and the share of the population behind each gate is stated; Vairam / Roy stays
  deferred (§2.9). Beside it, a per-target fraction descriptor and each target's share of the population (Buch alone
  holds 106 of the ≤ 214 frozen-11 units if its carrier qualifies). **≤ 214 is an upper bound** (v6, R10): a set built
  under a gate can fall below 5 columns (§1.6) and leave the population as "not testable (< 5)"; the < 5 outcomes of
  gated sets are reported when each gate opens, with the population and its composition restated. The TSV's figures: §4.

### 1.3 Inference level at the paper's final inferential step (amendment 4 §0.2)
One convention for every claim (the drafts proposed different ones; both are recorded where they differ):
- **spatial null** = a null on the map preserving spatial autocorrelation (spin, BrainSMASH, Moran spectral
  randomisation, variogram-matched surrogates);
- **gene null** = a test whose reference is built from genes outside S: random or permuted gene sets, gene-label
  permutation, GSEA / fGSEA, or an analytic S-versus-rest test (hypergeometric, Fisher, GeneOverlap, CSEA, Enrichr,
  ToppGene, Metascape, FUMA over-representation; Welch or Mann-Whitney of S against the rest; logistic regression of S
  membership); sub-labelled **uniform** (genes exchangeable, incl. within a restricted pool) or **structured** (matched
  or adjusted on coexpression, brain expression, gene length or trajectory);
- **none** = parametric tests that compare no set with other genes (a correlation across parcels, a regression across
  all genes of one gene-level statistic on another), or no test;
- **other** = a map-side null that does not preserve spatial autocorrelation (parcel- or region-label permutation,
  bootstrap of parcel values), a subject-label permutation, a parametric spatial model, or an unstated test.
- Nulls used upstream (e.g. spin inside list construction) or run and failed by the paper are recorded beside the level.
  Amendment 4 §1's table describes Pagani and Pretzsch as having "no spatial or gene null"; under this convention both
  are "gene null only (uniform)" (an analytic hypergeometric test is a uniform gene null). Both readings are recorded.
- Every registered cell's level is in the TSV, including the not-auditable ones.

### 1.4 Statistic T, direction, and what is not testable
- **Set-vs-map** (prereg §4): T = Pearson r(y, set score), set score = per-parcel mean of the set's genes on the §3.1
  matrix of the analysis class; Level 0 = parametric p of r; Spearman beside it.
- **Decoded-list enrichment** (amendment 4 §2): T = mean over S of the per-gene Pearson r between y and the gene's
  expression (= ⟨ŷ, s_z⟩); Level 0 = Welch t of S's per-gene r against the rest of the universe (prereg §4.7's
  enrichment Level 0); site leg recomputes every per-gene r on each surrogate; species leg per amendment 3 §2's
  enrichment rule (G1, G3m, G6l = mean over each null set, per-gene r and y fixed; G6s on the z-scored matrix; every null
  conditioned s_B(S) + s_W(R)).
- **PLS enrichment** (Buch, Vairam / Roy; prereg §4.7): T = mean PLS1 weight of S's genes, component oriented so
  corr(score, y) > 0; Level 0 Welch t; PLS1 refit on every surrogate.
- **Component level** (prereg §4.7; amendment 4 §2; amendment 5 §3): Level 0 (10,000 parcel-label permutations with
  refit; the parametric p of r for a fixed component) and the class-stratified site leg; read "passes / does not pass at
  component level", label "site leg only; not calibrated against expression-aligned maps"; never a survival.
- **Direction:** one-sided in the direction the paper reports; if the paper does not fix it, two-sided (p = 2·min,
  capped at 1), labelled (prereg §4.7). Only the paper's text, tables and figures fix a direction — never its code.
- **Not testable as stated** (the claim is registered, stays in the headline population as a non-survival with this
  flag, §1.2, and its T is computed as a descriptor where a registered T form exists — none for count or composition
  claims) when no registered T tests what the paper states:
  - a decoded or thresholded list that the paper states pools both signs of association (Segura; Romero-Garcia): a
    signed T cannot represent enrichment through both tails. A list whose sign composition the paper does not state
    keeps prereg §4.7's two-sided rule and is testable (Pagani, Park, Leyhausen), labelled "two-sided (sign composition
    unstated)";
  - a depletion of an unsigned list (RG-13, RG-14);
  - a genome-wide regression of per-gene statistics on per-gene fold changes (BENK-C2, PARK-C5), a tail-rank statistic
    (MOR-T1, MOR-T2), a count of significant regions (MOR-R1 … R4), a differential correlation between tissue cohorts
    (BE-01);
  - a signed-weight set score (Giacomel's combined-scheme and post-mortem-DEG cells; §2.3).
- **T_abs descriptor** (every decoded-list claim and every "not testable as stated" pooled list): mean over S of
  |per-gene r|, upper tail (lower tail for a stated depletion); Level 0 Welch t of |r|; site leg; unconditioned G1, G3m,
  G6l (G6s has no |r| form); each leg its own seed (§1.9). Labelled "unsigned descriptor, uncalibrated (no plant for
  |r|; |r| favours smooth, brain-enriched genes)"; its results are reported as values and p's, never as "detects" or
  "survives".

### 1.5 Reproduction (prereg §4.1; amendment 4 §2)
- **The §4.1 check applies where the paper reports a correlation-type value** (r, ρ, R², β, or a mean of per-gene
  correlations such as Patel's mean bicor): the paper's own functional recomputed on our matrix — Pearson for r,
  Spearman for ρ, R² with the paper's own model for R², the mean bicor for Patel, β for β; where the paper's score is
  weighted or its map is a derived statistic, that weighting or statistic (Giacomel: ρ of the |z|-weighted GEDAR score;
  Liu T3: ρ of the per-parcel rank-biserial enrichment of S; Romero-Garcia RG-01: r(PLS1 score, y) against the published
  r) — |ours − published| ≤ .05 → **reproduced**, else **not reproduced** (a sign opposite to the published one is "not
  reproduced (sign)"; no re-orientation after the fact). Only full-verdict claims leave the headline population when not
  reproduced (§1.2). A published value shown to rest on a defect is "not reproduced (documented source
  defect)", and the defect is reproduced as a descriptor.
- **Where the paper's statistic is not correlation-type** (OR, NES, p only, fold enrichment, dominance), the §4.1 check
  does not apply and the claim carries **"reproduction descriptive (amendment 4 §2)"**; it is neither excluded nor
  flagged (reading, §6 item 1: prereg §4.1's "reproduced claims only" is read as excluding claims that fail a defined
  check, since most frozen-11 claims report no correlation-type value). The descriptive comparison: the paper's decoded
  list re-derived by its own rule — the NeuroVault decoder output for the paper's image where the paper decoded with
  NeuroVault (fetched under the target's gate), else amendment 4 §2's per-donor parcel recipe, which is also the fallback
  if the decoder is unavailable — then the paper's enrichment test (its stated significance threshold; p < .05 where it
  states none); OR, p and Jaccard with any published list are reported.

### 1.6 Gene sets S (pinned now)
- **Mapping:** HGNC symbols (Ensembl IDs where given) to the §3.1 universe through
  `E:\data\asdg\explore_public\hgnc_complete_set\hgnc_complete_set_2026-09-04.txt`: approved symbol, then previous
  symbols, then aliases; a symbol matching two approved genes is dropped; every drop listed per claim. < 5 columns in the
  class universe → "not testable" (prereg §4).
- **Universe (v6, R5; the orchestrator under the operator's standing delegation, decided after the v5 S-build outcome was
  seen).** The universe of a class is its matrix's **15,633 columns**, as A1's calibrated engine and its plants use them. A
  set's members are all columns mapped to its HGNC genes (a gene with two columns contributes both). Unmapped columns stay
  in the universe (in the null pools) and can never be set members. The < 5 / > 1,000 rules count columns. Declared
  consequences: the **259 unmapped columns** (254 unmapped symbols, 4 ambiguous previous symbols, 1 ambiguous alias) are
  never members, and **63 of the built sets hold more columns than HGNC genes** (7 genes have two columns each); counting
  columns changes no < 5 or > 1,000 outcome of the v5 sets (§1.11 item 1). Pinned in the TSV header and
  `register_sets/MANIFEST.json`.
- **Name rule:** every GO, KEGG, MSigDB, Reactome and figure-label mapping follows §1.1's name rule (v6, R1), per cell.
- **Source order:** (1) the paper's own published list; (2) the primary publication the paper cites; (3) a versioned
  database at the release the paper names, else the release in force at the paper's submission date; a release that is
  no longer obtainable is replaced by the obtainable release closest in date (the earlier one on a tie). (2) and (3)
  are labelled **"S re-derived"** with the rule used, and "release substituted" where that applies. No threshold search:
  a pinned definition is applied once and its size reported beside the paper's. **Tool libraries (v6, R12):** where the
  paper names an enrichment tool whose dated library is obtainable, source 3 is that library at the release the paper
  used — Enrichr **KEGG_2021_Human** for Vairam / Roy (the library the paper names: "KEGG 2021 Human" via gseapy) and
  Enrichr **GO_Biological_Process_2015** for Romero-Garcia (the paper names Enrichr, version unstated; of the Enrichr GO BP
  libraries 2013, 2015, 2017, 2017b and 2018, only 2015 names both GO BP terms the paper prints — "Synaptic Transmission",
  "Membrane depolarization" — under the name rule, so it is read as the release the paper used; a reading taken after the
  libraries were read, declared); S = the library's gene list for the term as served (the GO annotation rule below does
  not apply to a library set); no "release substituted".
- **KEGG release rules (v6.1, Q2 / Q3; the orchestrator under the operator's standing delegation, after the v6 outcome was
  seen).** (i) A paper that ran KEGG through a tool library uses that library's release, identified by the vintage already
  registered for the same paper: Vairam / Roy → Enrichr KEGG_2021_Human (named); Romero-Garcia's KEGG families RG-03, RG-V06,
  RG-V07 → **(v6.2, P5; the orchestrator under the operator's standing delegation, after the v6.1 outcome was seen)** the
  candidates are Enrichr **KEGG_2015** and **KEGG_2016** (both served while its GO_Biological_Process_2015 was current; both
  pinned by sha256; KEGG_2016's terms read as "<name> Homo sapiens <hsa id>", the id split off as for the GO libraries'
  "(GO:id)"): the library that reaches **every** pathway name of the gated table by the name rule is used; both → KEGG_2015;
  neither → the one reaching more names; a tie → KEGG_2015; a name reached by **neither** → that cell "not auditable (S
  undefined)" (a name the chosen library misses but the other reaches takes the other's pathway — the reading of "reached by
  neither", declared); the family is not auditable if the gated table naming the cells is absent or no name reaches the
  chosen library. **(v6.3, O4)** A printed name carrying "[ _]Homo sapiens[ _]hsaNNNNN" is read with the suffix split off,
  and a printed hsa id selects KEGG_2016 (the pathway of that id where KEGG_2016 has it, else by the name rule); source 1 is
  looked for in one run per pathway with exactly one `--name` (several names for one run are refused, so no name alternative
  accumulates across pathways); **(v6.4, N4)** each run also takes the table's other printed pathway names as rival-only names
  (`--rival`; never alternatives), so "Ribosome" resolves to its own row beside "Ribosome biogenesis in eukaryotes". The choice is made under G1 (the names are sealed); the rule and seven plants on public
  library names run in the S-build (`MANIFEST.json` "kegg_release_rule" → "romerogarcia2019synaptic_families": 7 / 7
  caught; two with printed hsa ids). (ii) A paper naming KEGG
  itself — or a tool whose library release is not obtainable (Mamat: Metascape) — uses the obtainable KEGG release closest in
  date to its retrieval date, else its submission date, Enrichr snapshots counting as releases (a year-named snapshot dated
  to its year, its distance measured to the nearest day of that year, capped at the download date 2026-09-26; KEGG REST
  dated by its "Last update" 2026-09-25; ties to the earlier release; computed by the S-build, `MANIFEST.json`
  "kegg_release_rule"): **Leyhausen** (retrieved 2021-12-14) → Enrichr **KEGG_2021_Human** (0 days; next KEGG_2019_Human,
  714) — v6's reading that kept it on KEGG REST because the paper "names KEGG itself" is removed; **Mamat** (received
  2024-08-14) → Enrichr **KEGG_2026** (505 days to 2026-01-01; KEGG REST 772 days) — KEGG REST would be closer only if
  KEGG_2026 had been built on the download day itself (farthest reading 773 days), declared. **(v6.7 (2), the uniform archive
  rule)** The complete KEGG REST releases the Wayback Machine holds count as releases too (a day on which both
  `link/hsa/pathway` and `list/pathway/hsa` were captured, HTTP 200; `kegg_archived_releases`): **Mamat → KEGG REST as
  captured 2024-09-16** (33 days; next the capture of 2023-05-31, 441 days; Enrichr KEGG_2026, 505) — its cells stay under
  G1, only their rule text changes; **Leyhausen** unchanged (KEGG_2021_Human, 0 days).
- **Corroboration of source 1 (v6.5, (1); the orchestrator under the operator's standing delegation, decided after five review
  rounds — v6.0 → v6.4 — each found a new text layout through which a result list becomes S, so the text criterion alone
  could not exclude result lists).** A carrier list that passes the text criterion below (now a **first filter**) is source 1
  only if it is ALSO corroborated: **(a)** where the register pins an external definition of the same set (sources 2–3),
  Jaccard(the list's HGNC genes in the cell's class universe, the external set's HGNC genes in that universe) ≥ .80 — against
  the set built now in `register_sets` (Buch's 27 GO-slim fallbacks for its 108 GO-slim cells; **since v6.6, B2, also the
  comparators of Buch's Fig. 5c–e sets and of Pagani's sets**, below) or, for the Romero-Garcia KEGG pathways, against the
  pathway the engine builds itself from the pinned Enrichr library by §1.6's KEGG family rule (**v6.6, B1**: the "recipe";
  v6.5's `--external`, a file supplied at gate time, is withdrawn); **(b)** where no external definition is pinned, the number
  of **distinct approved HGNC symbols** of the list (v6.6, C1: not its rows; the S-build's HGNC rule) equals the paper's
  printed size for that cell (pinned in `carrier_tests.json` "source1_lists" → "cells"; the Zhang cells, 500 — the one "500"
  Romero-Garcia prints: "We defined cell-type specific genes as the top 500 genes with higher expression in the cell-type
  compared to the remaining five genes.", Materials and methods, "Genetic modules and enrichment analyses", Mol Psychiatry
  24:1053, p. 1056 (PDF page 4 of 12; the article's text holds no other "500"), pinned with the PDF's sha256 130a9b6c… as
  "printed_size_source"); **(c)** otherwise the
  else branch — and for a cell with neither an external definition nor a printed size it is **"not auditable (S undefined:
  source 1 uncorroborated)"**, never the text criterion alone. **A leading-edge, overlap, hit or core-enrichment list is a
  strict subset of its set, so it fails (a) and (b) by construction** (a strict subset has Jaccard |list| / |set| with its set,
  below .80 unless it keeps more than 80 % of it, and a length below the printed size). The universes are
  `register_sets/UNIVERSE_GENES.json` (the HGNC genes with columns in each class; written by the S-build); the HGNC mapping is
  the S-build's. This **supersedes v6.1–v6.4's statement that "a results table is refused whatever its caption says"**: the
  text criterion stays as a first filter. Its residual gap (v6.5, declared; restated by v6.6 C6): a result list still becomes
  S only if it passes the text filter AND keeps ≥ 80 % of the external set in the universe (a leading edge covering nearly the
  whole set) — worked example, a plant (the reviewer's A7): an 85 % subset (96 of the 112 in-universe genes) of Buch's
  "secretory vesicle" comparator in an input-worded sheet qualifies (Jaccard .857) — or, for the Zhang cells, carries exactly
  500 distinct approved symbols (a result ranking of exactly 500 genes).
  **(v6.6, B1) Pinned comparators.** Every external comparator is pinned per cell in `carrier_tests.json` ("corroboration":
  set id, the committed per-set JSON's sha256 and its members' sha256 — SHA-256 of the sorted `members_hgnc` joined by LF; the
  KEGG families: the recipe with both Enrichr libraries' sha256). The engine reads a comparator only from the committed
  `register_sets` folder and refuses a file whose set id, class universe or either sha256 differs from the pins; every read,
  parse or mismatch error sends the cell to the else branch with its reason ("comparator refused: …"), and the record names
  the set id and hashes it used. Plants (§1.11 item 8): the genuine PAG-C1 list against its pinned comparator qualifies; the
  same list with another committed set in its place, a self-made set (with and without its own file hash pinned), a
  missing file, malformed JSON, a byte appended to the genuine file and a class the set has no universe for are each
  refused; the RG-03 recipe qualifies the pathway list its rule picks and refuses a changed library pin and a name that
  reaches no pathway. The builder asserts every pin against the committed files. **(v6.7, N3)** The class universes
  (`UNIVERSE_GENES.json`) and the HGNC table the engine reads are pinned too (`carrier_tests.json` "corroboration":
  `universe_genes_sha256`, `hgnc_table` path + sha256, `universe_min_genes` 10,000), asserted and recorded inside the same
  try / except that routes every read, parse or pin mismatch to the else branch (the record reads "the pinned universe /
  HGNC table could not be used (…)"). Plants: a universe restricted to the 30 leading-edge genes (its pin unchanged; and
  with its own hash pinned: under 10,000 genes), a missing universe file and a changed HGNC pin each send Secretory
  vesicle's genuine list to the else branch, recorded; the builder asserts both pins against the files.
  **(v6.6, C4) The Jaccard's universe is ours, not the paper's (declared).** It is computed within our class universe
  (`UNIVERSE_GENES.json`: 15,367 HGNC genes over the four classes; V-AAL for Buch and Pagani) — not the paper's (Buch: the
  10,438 AHBA genes of its preprocessing; Pagani: 16,796 brain-expressed genes). A genuine input list the paper filtered to
  its own universe can therefore miss .80 against a comparator that is not so filtered: a **structural refusal** (the safe
  direction — the cell takes the comparator, labelled "S re-derived"). And "a leading-edge, overlap, hit or core-enrichment
  list fails by construction" holds only where the comparator equals the paper's own set: against a comparator that differs
  from it (another release, another threshold), a result list could reach .80 only by covering ≥ 80 % of the comparator in our
  universe — the residual above.
  **(v6.6, C8) Declared deviation from the frozen A1 prereg wording.** `prereg.md` (FROZEN a632b852…) asks what fraction of
  claims survives the cascade "applied to the authors' own maps and gene sets" (its Q-primary, lines 16–17) and reserves
  "not auditable" for "a target without its public material" (line 211). Rule (c) — a cell with neither an external
  definition nor a printed size is "not auditable (S undefined: source 1 uncorroborated)" even if the authors' own list lies
  in the (public, gated) carrier — and v6.6's "not auditable (S undefined: comparator not buildable pre-freeze)" depart from
  both: an authors' list that may exist is not used, and the cell is not auditable although its material may be public;
  (a) and (b) likewise set an uncorroborated authors' list aside (the cell then takes the comparator, "S re-derived").
  **Why:** after five review rounds (v6.0 → v6.4) no text rule could keep a result list (a leading edge, an overlap, a
  hit list) from being taken for the set it tests — the circularity this audit exists to catch — so the orchestrator, under
  the operator's standing delegation, closed the class structurally (v6.5 (1)): a carrier list is used only when a definition
  or a size fixed before the freeze corroborates it. **Cells affected** (corroboration mode none, 41 since v6.8; 37 in
  v6.7, 41 in v6.6): rule (c) — BUCH-E-Trans-StructConstRibosome-S1 … S4, RG-N03, RG-15, RG-V23 (v6.5); no buildable
  comparator — the 32 Buch cells of ASD downregulated, ASDup, FMRP, Grove, CNS autoimmune, psoriasis, vocal learning and
  Tourette's and PAG-C9, PAG-C10 (v6.6; the four ASDdown cells left the list in v6.7, N1, and returned in v6.8, B S1: the name
  "asdM12" reaches M12 only through a reference not cited for the set); 24 of the 41 are claims (22 of Buch —
  BUCH-E-Trans-StructConstRibosome-S1 and the 21 of v6.8 — and PAG-C9, PAG-C10), 15 descriptor cells, 2 family rows (RG-15,
  RG-V23). The deviation is declared here for the operator's confirmation at the freeze (or a frozen amendment).
- **Comparators built before the freeze — the resolution rule (v6.6, B2; the orchestrator under the operator's standing
  delegation; this rule was written into the register before any of the comparators below was downloaded or built).**
  Buch's non-GO sets (the Methods' "Gene set lists": refs 19, 61, 117–129) and Pagani's sets (Gandal 2022 modules, ref. 16;
  Reactome; SynGO) need no map and no gated carrier, so §1.6 requires them built now; they are the else-branch S of their
  cells and the comparators of corroboration (a). One rule resolves what the papers leave open: **(R-a) source** — the
  article or database the paper cites for the set, in the form the source distributes it (a supplementary table, a table in
  the article, a database download); **(R-b) release** — for a versioned or updated source, the release current at the paper's
  first submission date (the "Received" date the article prints: Buch 2021-11-29, Pagani 2025-04-17), if that release is
  archived and obtainable; else the earliest archived release after that date; if no dated release is obtainable at all, the
  release obtainable at download ("release substituted", §1.6's closest-obtainable rule); **(R-c) threshold** — the paper's
  own threshold where it states one; else the source's own documented default (named per set); where the source documents
  none, every gene the named list or term carries as distributed; a term of an ontology takes its annotations to the term and
  its descendants where the source's own gene report does so; **(R-d) names** — the paper's set name maps to the source's
  term by the name rule (§1.1); **(R-e) not buildable** — a source reachable only with a personal identifier or a login, or
  needing gated material, or whose list cannot be read unambiguously from what it distributes, is not built: corroboration
  mode none, and the cells read "not auditable (S undefined: comparator not buildable pre-freeze)". Each built set records
  its URL, release / version, access date, threshold and sha256 in `register_sets/MANIFEST.json`
  ("comparators_v6_6") and its per-set JSON.
- **The resolution rule applied, and its outcome per set (v6.6, B2 / C5, as revised by v6.7; the orchestrator under the
  operator's standing delegation; readings taken while building, declared — the rule's text above is unchanged, its sha256
  43cefdcc… asserted by the builder).** C5: Buch's Methods cite a source for every one of its 24 Fig. 5c–e sets (p. 31, "Gene
  set lists"; refs 19, 61, 118–129) — v6.5 read nine of them (MSA, dementia, heart disease, psoriasis, ID, aphasia, conduct
  disorder, antisocial PD, neurotic disorder) as sourceless, wrongly. **Readings of the rule:** (i) **R-b on web-archive
  captures** — the Internet Archive's Wayback Machine (CDX listings and raw captures, served without an account) archives
  SFARI Gene's genes CSV, the SPARK gene list, DISEASES' weekly files and (v6.7) RGD's and CTD's download files and KEGG
  REST; a capture on or before the date shows the release current at it only if a capture after the date carries the same
  content digest; else the earliest capture after the date is "the earliest archived release after that date" (v6.6; **taken
  while building, outcome known**); a source distributed as several files (DISEASES' channels; CTD's gene-disease file and
  vocabulary) has a release only as a set of files of one date (v6.6 for DISEASES, v6.7 for CTD; **taken while building,
  outcome known** — for CTD it changed the outcome: the alternative reading, the gene-disease file alone, gives its earliest
  capture after the date, **2023-12-05**, instead of the 2024-02-10 set; computed, `archive_reading_v6_8` of the CTD comparator's
  record); a capture the crawler cut short (RGD) is not an obtainable release (v6.7; **taken while building, outcome known** —
  the alternative, the earliest capture after the date, is 2023-06-06, cut short at 1 MiB); a complete archived KEGG REST
  release is a day on which both `link/hsa/pathway` and `list/pathway/hsa` were captured (v6.7; **taken while building,
  outcome known**). (v6.8 C: these tags added; the readings are unchanged.) **(i′) v6.7 N2 — a clarification
  of (i) made after the v6.6 build (post hoc: known which cells it affects; no carrier or result seen; taken while building,
  outcome known):** where the source
  serves a release label (SFARI Gene: the Content-Disposition file name "SFARI-Gene_genes_<release>release_<export>export.csv"),
  R-b reads the **served release label**, not the digest alone: the release current at the date is the latest release a label
  names with a release date on or before it, and the comparator is the **state current at the date** — the most recent capture
  at or before the date that serves that release (none archived → the earliest state after the date, "state substituted").
  (ii) **R-d**: the paper's names for a set are its Methods name and its figure row label; each is tried by the name rule;
  the set is named iff at least one reaches a term and all that reach one reach the same term. (iii) **R-c / R-e**: a source
  presenting exactly one gene list (by its title or column) gives that list; a source distributing several lists and naming
  none as the set, or loci / statistics instead of a list, cannot be read unambiguously (R-e); an annotation row qualified
  "no_association" (RGD) is not an association. Sources fetched by `src_register/fetch_v66.py` after the rule was written
  (`E:\data\asdg\register_sets_src\<source>\SOURCE.md`: URL, date, sha256, licence); each set's record (source, URL, release
  and the rule's outcome, threshold, names and the name rule's records, access date 2026-09-27, every input's sha256) is in
  `register_sets/MANIFEST.json` "comparators_v6_6" and its per-set JSON. **Outcome (v6.9): 35 comparator records, 24 built
  (70 cells), 11 not buildable (38 cells)** (v6.8: 25 / 74, 10 / 34; v6.7: 26 / 78, 9 / 30; v6.6: 25 / 74, 10 / 34) — HGNC
  genes / V-AAL columns:

  Consequences: m 230 → 207 (v6.6) → 211 (v6.7: the four ASDdown claims return, N1) → **207 (v6.8, B S1: they leave again —
  R-d applied uniformly)**, unchanged by v6.9; the 21 Buch claims of the six unbuildable sets with claims (ASDdown ×4, ASDup
  ×4, FMRP ×4, CNS autoimmune ×4, vocal learning ×3, Tourette's ×2) and PAG-C9, PAG-C10 are not auditable, S undefined (§4);
  the descriptor cells of Grove, psoriasis and (v6.9, C5) ASD rare de novo are not auditable, S undefined, and count in no m;
  Buch's GAD, antisocial PD and neurotic disorder comparators have < 5 columns, so their 12 descriptor cells are "not testable
  (< 5)"; the 70 cells with a built comparator (v6.8: 74, RDNV's four among them; v6.7: 78; v6.6: 74 — its text said 108, the
  number of all Buch Fig. 5c–e and Pagani cells; corrected) are corroborated against it (mode external) and take it as their
  else-branch S. The Gandal module labels were transcribed by eye (v6.6); an independent blind
  re-transcription (the v6.6 re-check) matches every label, and they are kept.
- **The uniform archive rule (v6.7 (2); the orchestrator under the operator's standing delegation: the uniform application
  of reading (i) of the rule fixed above, not a new rule — declared as such).** The Wayback Machine answered again during
  the v6.6 build, so every register cell whose comparator had been ruled "no archived release" or "release substituted" for
  want of an archive was re-examined with the same archive rule (exact-URL CDX listings, raw "id_" captures, served lengths
  and labels by HEAD requests). **Cells examined, and the outcome:** (1) **Liu LIU-T1 … T6's merged set** (SFARI score 1;
  §2.10) — the §1.6 SFARI rule on the 17 archived states read by their served labels (`sfari_wayback_captures/`,
  `served_labels.json`) and the current download: the earliest release whose score-1 count equals Liu's 233 is **03-28-2024**
  (its earliest state, the export of 2024-05-15; release 01-16-2024 has 232) — built (the merged set: 258 HGNC genes / 249
  columns; v6.6: release 2026-07-12, "release substituted", score 1 = 245); **changed**. (2) **Xing XING-FPV-all / hc / syn**
  (SFARI all / score 1 / syndromic) — no archived or current state has all = 1,277; the last release before the paper's date
  is **07-12-2026**, whose only obtainable state is the export of 2026-09-25: "state substituted" (all 1,289, score 1 245,
  syndromic 312 — the same files as v6.6, the label changed from "release substituted"); **release confirmed, state
  substituted**. **Xing's date (v6.8 C, declared; the orchestrator under the operator's standing delegation, after the v6.7
  final re-check):** its first journal submission, **2026-08-31** (the Research Square In-Review timeline, "first submitted to
  journal 31 Aug 2026", `literature/notes/xing2026interpretable.md`), not the preprint's posted date 2026-09-18 that v6.7 read;
  the release and the state are the same, the state now 25 days after the date (computed in the S-build). (3) **Buch SFARIsyn ×4** — by (i′): release 09-02-2021, the export
  of 10-22-2021 (143 syndromic) instead of v6.6's export of 01-24-2022 of the same release (230); **changed**. **The
  within-release change, declared (N2):** the two exports serve the same release, yet 87 genes flip syndromic 0 → 1 and one
  gene's score changes between them (no gene added or removed). (4) **Buch osteoarthritis, MSA, dementia ×4 each** (RGD) —
  captures of RGD's human annotation file after 2021-11-29: 2023-06-06 and 2024-05-26 cut short by the crawler (1 MiB of
  ~130–340 MB), **2024-07-24 complete** ("date-generated: 2024-07-20"), with RDO_20240720 from RGD's own weekly ontology
  archive — built; **changed** (v6.6's single capture examined came from a query limited to 2019–2023). CNS autoimmune stays
  not buildable (R-d). (5) **Buch schizophrenia ×4** (CTD) — v6.6's "not in the Wayback Machine" came from a malformed CDX
  query (a wildcard mid-URL); exact-URL listings show captures; the earliest day after the date with a gene-disease file and
  its disease vocabulary both captured is **2024-02-10** (report created 2024-01-31) — built; **changed** (by reading (i)'s
  "set of files of one date", taken while building with the outcome known; the gene-disease file alone would give its
  capture of 2023-12-05 — named v6.8 C). (6) **Mamat's gated
  KEGG family** — KEGG rule (ii) with the complete archived KEGG REST releases counted: KEGG REST as captured 2024-09-16 (33
  days) instead of Enrichr KEGG_2026 (505) — **changed** (text only; the cells stay under G1). (7) **Leyhausen's KEGG cells**
  — KEGG_2021_Human, 0 days: unchanged. (8) **Rasero's WebGestalt categories** (the 2022 server release; §2.6) — the server's
  gene-set API is not archived; unchanged ("current server"). (9) **Zhang 2014's top-500 lists** (RG-04 …) — the rule does not
  determine a set from sources 2–3 (no "top 500" list is published) and the journal supplement is not archived: unchanged.

- **The registered secondary view "near-miss comparator" (v6.7 (1); the orchestrator under the operator's standing
  delegation; fixed now, before G1 opens, and declared as chosen knowing which cells it affects).** The strict rule stays the
  **primary** view: m = 207 and the 23 claims above stay "not auditable (S undefined: comparator not buildable pre-freeze)" —
  the rule is not relaxed. **v6.8 (B S1; the orchestrator under the operator's standing delegation, decided after the v6.7
  final re-check — post hoc, declared):** v6.7 N1 had put asdM12 in the primary view through a name bridge ("asdM12" → M12)
  that only Parikshak 2013 supplies, a reference Buch does not cite for this set — a looser standard than the one that sends
  Tourette's (a normalisation), CNS autoimmune and PAG-C9 / C10 (text descriptions) to this view. R-d is now applied
  uniformly: ASD downregulated is not buildable in the primary view and its set (Voineagu's Table 14) is kept built and
  pinned as the view's comparator, so the focal BUCH-E-ASDdown-S1 is auditable in this view only (§0). **v6.9 (the
  orchestrator under the operator's standing delegation, decided after the v6.8 skeptic re-check — post hoc, declared):**
  (C5) reading (iii) is applied uniformly — a source presenting several lists gives one only by its title or column, and
  Sanders 2019's Table S1 names no list "rare de novo" (v6.6 … v6.8 took ASD65 by a semantic reading of its column note), the
  standard that sends PAG-C9 / C10 (lists reached by description) to this view: ASD rare de novo is not buildable in the
  primary view and ASD65's 65 genes are its view comparator; its four cells are descriptor cells, so m and m′ are unchanged.
  (C3) The label names the bridge: **"comparator from a near-miss source (not the cited reference) / normalised name /
  name bridge / note reading"** (v6.7 … v6.8 without "/ name bridge"; the ASDdown comparator is Voineagu's own list reached
  through a name bridge, neither a near-miss source nor a normalisation; v6.9 without "/ note reading" — **v6.10, post hoc,
  declared:** a **note reading** is a list of the cited source reached through a note or its text, not its title or printed
  label: RDNV's ASD65 through its column note, and PAG-C9 / C10's modules through Gandal 2022's Results text, which v6.7 …
  v6.9 filed under "near-miss source" although Gandal 2022 is the reference Pagani cites). Each route has its term: ASDup,
  FMRP — near-miss source; Tourette's (and CNS autoimmune's attempt) — normalised name; ASDdown — name bridge; RDNV, PAG-C9,
  PAG-C10 — note reading.
  Beside it, for exactly the cells whose comparator is not buildable and whose set a near-miss source, a declared name
  normalisation, (v6.8) a name bridge from a reference not cited for the set or (v6.10) a list of the cited source reached
  through a note or its text rather than its title reaches, the view builds a **comparator from a near-miss source (not the cited reference) / normalised name
  / name bridge / note reading** — so labelled wherever it is reported — by the same resolution rule; it never changes a
  primary status; its sets have no primary cells (`MANIFEST.json` "comparators_v6_6" → "near_miss_view_v6_7"; pinned per cell
  in `carrier_tests.json` "corroboration_views" → "near_miss", set id + both sha256; run with `carrier_test.py source1 --view
  near_miss`); the TSV gives each such row two columns (`secondary_view_near_miss_status`, `secondary_view_near_miss_set_id`)
  and the view its own **m′ = 223** (§4). **The declared normalisation**, applied to these two names only: strip the
  possessive "'s" ("Tourette's syndrome" → "Tourette syndrome", "Tourette's" → "Tourette"); expand "CNS" to "central nervous
  system". **The names:** Parikshak 2013 introduced "asdM12" / "asdM16" for Voineagu 2011's modules — "We obtained asdM12 and
  adsM16 from a prior, independent gene expression study that profiled expression changes in ASD cortex and applied WGCNA to
  identify modules of dysregulated genes ASD (Voineagu et al., 2011)" (article, Experimental Procedures, "Gene sets"; its
  Extended Experimental Procedures: "We obtained asdM12 (444 genes) and adsM16 (386 genes) …"); Voineagu's own text says M12 /
  M16 and never "asdM" (asserted in the S-build). **Per set:**

  | cells | near-miss comparator | outcome (HGNC / V-AAL) |
  |---|---|---|
  | BUCH-E-ASDdown-S1 … S4 (4 claims; the focal S1 among them) — v6.8, B S1 | Voineagu 2011 Supplementary Information, "Supplementary Table 14. M12 module genes and associated GWAS p values" (pp. 19–23; p. 10 maps M12 to the pink module, M16 to tan): the cited source's own list, reached from the paper's name "asdM12" only through Parikshak 2013's naming (a near-miss **name bridge**; set `nm68_buch_voineagu2011_M12_table14`, the same membership as v6.7's `cmp67_…`). **Declared (v6.8 C):** the table lists 380 gene symbols while p. 10 prints 432 for M12 (asserted by `s_extract.py` when the extract was made, v6.7; the PDF has since been deleted) — the table may list only the genes with a GWAS p value (its caption), not verified; the comparator is the table as distributed. Overlap with Parikshak 2013's "asdM12 from Voineagu et al 2011" (444 rows, 421 distinct symbols), **recomputed by the S-build (v6.8 C)**: 312 symbols shared; by the §1.6 HGNC rule 377 and 420 genes, **364 shared**; in the V-AAL universe **Jaccard .842** (358 of 425) — the record `overlap_with_parikshak2013_asdM12` (so Parikshak 2013's list would corroborate against this comparator, ≥ .80; a descriptor, declared); none of its symbols in Parikshak 2013's "asdM16" | built, 377 / 370 |

  | BUCH-E-RDNV-S1 … S4 (4 descriptor cells) — v6.9, C5 | Sanders 2019 Table S1, column ASD65 = 1: the cited source's column, reached from "ASD rare de novo" through its note ("If the gene is associated with autism spectrum disorder: Sanders et al. Neuron 2015"), not its title (set `nm69_buch_sanders2019_asd65`, the same 65 genes as v6.6 … v6.8's primary `cmp66_buch_sanders2019_asd65`); descriptor cells — not in m′ | built, 65 / 61 |
  | BUCH-E-CNSauto-S1 … S4 (4 claims) | "central nervous system autoimmune" (after the normalisation) reaches no RDO term (RDO_20240720) | **not buildable either** — not auditable in both views |
  | BUCH-E-Vocal-S1 … S4 (claims S1, S3, S4) | — (N4: the tables were obtained; the cited source itself distributes the candidate lists, and picking one is the choice R-e forbids; the instruction added the claims to the view only if the tables stayed unobtainable) | **not buildable either** — not auditable in both views |
  | PAG-C9 (claim) | Gandal 2022 **GeneM3** — **N6:** its Results text: "These included GeneM3, a neuronal module enriched for energy generation and neuronal processes that are highly energy dependent, such as vesicle transport and release" (every content word of "vesicle transport and release" in it). **v6.8 C:** found by a search, not by hand — `s_build_v66.content_word_search` reads every sentence of Gandal's article page that names exactly one gene module and holds every content word of the name ("and" dropped; singular / plural); exactly one module is found (asserted), the one v6.7 had picked | built, 682 / 669 |
  | PAG-C10 (claim) | Gandal 2022 **GeneM5** — "GeneM5 is downregulated in ASD, contains many neuronal genes involved in synaptic vesicle function, cytoskeleton and synaptic plasticity" (every content word of "synaptic vesicles and plasticity", singular / plural normalised); v6.8 C: found by the same search, exactly one module (asserted) | built, 396 / 392 |

  Pagani used "co-expression modules of known biological function (n = 10)" of ref. 16 (its Methods; quoted from the
  full text); the figure labels are therefore not the only module descriptions its source gives — `gandal2022_module_labels.json`
  "the only module names the source gives" is corrected (N6). asdM12 left the view in v6.7 (N1: primary) and returned in v6.8
  (B S1). **m′ = 207 + 16 = 223** (ASDdown ×4, ASDup ×4, FMRP ×4, TS S1 / S4, PAG-C9, PAG-C10; v6.7: 211 + 12); CNS
  autoimmune ×4 and vocal learning ×3 stay out in both views; RDNV's four descriptor cells (v6.9) count in neither m nor m′.
  The view: 9 set records (v6.8: 8), 7 built (22 cells), 2 not buildable (8 cells).
- **Very wide comparators and printed counts (v6.7 N8 / N9; declared).** Corroboration against a very wide comparator is
  structurally near-impossible (a source-1 list reaches Jaccard ≥ .80 only by covering ≥ 80 % of it): **CTD schizophrenia**
  covers 14,607 of the 15,374 mapped columns (95.0 %; v6.6's 2026 release 97.5 %) — its four cells are descriptors; the
  **DISEASES ADHD union** has 1,280 columns (genes per channel: knowledge 0, experiments 1,209, text mining 375) and covers
  **2 claims in m** (BUCH-E-ADHD-S1, S4). **Pua 2016's counts:** the table prints "n = 169 + 5" genes; the comparator has 132
  distinct genes from 212 rows (genes recur across conditions); the HCM and DCM headers print 48 / 51 genes against 47 / 50
  rows — declared, the table taken as distributed (the per-set JSON records every condition's printed and listed count).
- **The evidence that the rule preceded the downloads (v6.7 N5; declared).** The rule was written at 2026-09-27T02:56:44Z;
  the first comparator source was downloaded at 03:21:39Z (`steinberg2013_fmrp/mmc1.pdf`); the target paper's accepted
  manuscript (`buch2023_aam.pdf`, read for its Methods' "Gene set lists" and references) was downloaded at **02:52:40Z,
  before the rule** (v6.6's `fetch_v66.py` docstring said "before any file below was downloaded" — corrected). The order
  rests on this box's file times and the register's own time stamp: it is **self-attested**, and rule, downloads and builds
  landed in **one commit** (b5335d1), so no outside record witnesses it.
- **Individual-level data in supplements (v6.7 (3); the handling recorded, and the rule going forward).** While locating the
  gene-level tables for v6 … v6.6, supplements holding per-individual or per-sample data were opened and a few of their rows
  were **displayed once in a model context** before the files were deleted: Parikshak 2013 supplement 03 (TableS2C — its
  header row and one row of per-individual variants — and rows of TableS2D); Pua 2016 ESM1 (rows of the per-sample sheets ST5 /
  ST6); Gandal 2022 MOESM3 ("datMeta_datSeq", 3 rows), MOESM9 ("MethylCTD_Props", 2 rows) and MOESM10 ("snRNAseq_metadata", 3
  rows); Parikshak 2016 Supplementary Table 1 (two header cells of TableS1a); beside them the v6.6 re-check printed the header
  rows of Parikshak 2016 Supplementary Tables S2c / S3c (published brain-bank sample IDs) once to its console and then deleted
  the files. **Kept — v6.7's statement, corrected by v6.8 (B S2; the orchestrator under the operator's standing delegation,
  after the v6.7 final re-check):** v6.7 wrote that only gene-level extracts and whole workbooks with no identifier-bearing
  sheet were kept. That was inaccurate: its guard read identifier *wording* only, and three whole workbooks it kept held
  identifier-bearing sheets — Kang 2011 MOESM50 (Supplementary Tables 1–4: donor codes; Tables 15–16 wider than 50 columns),
  Parikshak 2013 supplement 02 (TableS1B: BrainSpan sample codes as its column headers; TableS1C: per-sample metadata;
  TableS1A itself 84 columns wide) and Parikshak 2016 Supplementary Table 2 (TableS2c: brain-bank sample codes; TableS2a and
  TableS2d wider than 50 columns). **v6.8:** each was reduced to the gene-level content the builders read
  (`src_register/reduce_v68.py`: a JSON sheet dump beside the original — Kang, "Supple Table 9." whole, a clean sheet;
  Parikshak 2013, TableS1A's three columns "ENSEMBL GENE ID", "Gene Symbol", "Module Label"; Parikshak 2016, the five TableS2a
  columns the S-build reads), the builders were pointed at the reductions and their outputs shown byte-identical before the
  originals were deleted (every per-set JSON and the four tables of `register_sets/`; `MANIFEST.json` equal but for the
  sha256 of the builders' own source files; the Kang extract identical), and the originals were deleted — their sha256, bytes
  and sheet names and dimensions are kept in the table below, in `extract_manifest.json` ("originals_deleted_v68") and in
  each folder's SOURCE.md. No row or header cell of an identifier-bearing sheet was displayed in this work — only sheet
  names, dimensions, the guard's rule letters and pattern counts, and, to diagnose accession-like false positives in other
  workbooks (not the three), the letter / digit shapes (e.g. "A99999") and positions of matched tokens, never a value.
  **Round 2 (v6.8; the orchestrator under the operator's standing delegation, on round 1's report — one uniform rule, no
  per-flag judgement):** every flagged workbook a register builder reads was reduced by the same script to **exactly the
  sheets and columns the builders read** — Gandal 2018 Table S1, Polioudakis 2019 Table S4, Satterstrom 2020 Table S2, Lake
  2018's Supplementary Tables, Gandal 2022 Supplementary Data 5 (MOESM7) and 6 (MOESM8), Sanders 2019 Tables S1 / S2 / S4, and
  Pfenning 2014 Tables S1–S8 (a zip of eight workbooks of which the builders read the sheet names only: the names kept, no
  cell); a reduction returns each kept column at its original position, so the builders' code is the original's; the
  builders' outputs were byte-identical with the reductions read and the originals still present (every per-set JSON and the
  four tables; `MANIFEST.json` but for the builders' own sha256; the two extracts `fetch_v66.py` makes from MOESM7 and Sanders
  2019, rebuilt in memory, identical), and the eight originals were then deleted (table below). One reduction was still
  flagged by the v6.8 guard — Sanders 2019's seven columns, by an accession-like header ("CHD253", the source's own flag
  name): it is read in declared columns only (v6.9: so is Lake 2018's register reduction, which the v6.9 guard flags V — its
  builder already declared the two columns; outputs unchanged). Round 2 left whole two workbooks no builder reads (sha256
  only: Parikshak 2016 Supplementary Table 4, Voineagu 2011 Supplementary Table 2) and did not touch the two exploration-lane
  files (Velmeshev 2019 Data S4, whose exploration ingest loaded every sheet — the per-individual "Individual_specific_DEGs"
  among them — to record its dimensions; Lake 2018's MOESM11 in `explore_public`, the register copy's source): both are v6.9's
  (below). Kang 2011's round-1 reduction keeps "Supple Table 9." whole (17 columns, clean) while round 2 keeps declared
  columns only — the one difference in form, declared.
  **v6.9 (the orchestrator under the operator's standing delegation, on the v6.8 skeptic re-check 0 A / 2 B / 5 C — post
  hoc, declared).** **(B, codes of real formats.)** v6.8's `privacy_guard.py` — its docstring, its self-test plants and its
  fake .xls sheet — and this section's example of a number joined to a region code carried participant / sample codes of
  real formats, written by a model worker: 13 families (BrainSpan, NeuroBioBank and brain-bank "AN" donor codes, codes of a
  number joined to a region, a brain-bank prefix code, short letter + digit codes, NDA GUID, SSC, SPARK, GEO sample, GTEx
  donor, TCGA participant, BIDS subject label; 28 occurrences in the guard, 1 here). They are replaced by synthetic codes
  (digit runs all 9 or all 0, invented letter parts), are not reproduced here and were not looked up. **What was searched
  (v6.10, B NEW-PII-residual — v6.9 said "a search of the programme … found no other code of these formats", which was
  wider than the search):** this register, its builders (`src_register/`), `register_maps/`, `register_sets/`, the TSV and
  its builder, and every file listed in `register_freeze_manifest.json` hold no code of these families but the synthetic

  `git grep` at HEAD 2f79460, synthetic all-9 / all-0 forms excluded):** BIDS-format subject labels of ≥ 3 digits — 14 in
  A3's test code (`arcs/replication/src/tests/`, 2 files; the ABIDE-I-range label v6.8's fixtures also used is one of the

  matches of the GTEx pattern in `literature/notes/` are prose words — the consortium's name, a hyphen and letters only —
  not donor codes). These occurrences (A3 code, tests and
  logs; literature notes) are outside this register, not edited by this revision, and referred to a programme-level
  privacy review outside this register (the orchestrator). The orchestrator records the incident and decides on the git
  history (not changed by this revision).
  **(S2-residual: the exploration-lane copies of two register-source workbooks — the same rule.)** **Velmeshev 2019 Data S4** (487,936 B, 53c9b418…): every sheet
  classified by the v6.9 guard, rule letters only — all four clean, "Individual_specific_DEGs" (1,072 × 6) among them —; its

  `explore/discovery/data_manifests/celltype_velmeshev2019_S4_dimensions.json` (no cell); reduced to what code reads —

  **(C1) The guard:** its header zone is the first **20** rows (v6.8: 6); a sheet's width is its **populated** width, not
  the workbook's dimension tag; the value patterns also take ".", "-" and space as separators, NDA GUIDs written NDAR_INV… /
  NDARINV…, the wording "brain bank ID", and a bare subject word (Individual, Donor, Subject, Patient, Sample, Proband,
  Participant) heading a column of numbers or short codes. Re-run over every workbook a builder reads or pins
  (`privacy_inventory_v68.json`, under the programme venv): no builder-read workbook newly flagged but Lake 2018's register
  reduction (V, read in declared columns already). **Correction (a dimension-tag artefact):** Parikshak 2016 Supplementary
  Table 4's "TableS4a W" rested on openpyxl's dimension tag (53 columns); its populated width is 8 — clean. The v6.9 guard
  flags that workbook's TableS4b V instead (164 × 4) — unread, left whole (sha256 only). The widths in the table below are
  v6.8's records of the deleted originals — openpyxl's dimension tag for an .xlsx (it may exceed the populated width), xlrd's
  column count for an .xls — and their verdicts v6.8's six-row guard; the originals are deleted and cannot be re-read. On
  the one file still readable in v6.9 (Lake 2018, the exploration copy) the v6.9 guard flags more than v6.8 recorded for the
  register copy — Table S3 W V, Table S9 N, Table S10 N V besides Table S1 V — and the tags overstated two widths (Table S6
  28 populated of 32, Table S12 3 of 26): the table's v6.9 row. **v6.10 (C1-width, C1-coverage):** the guard reads an
  openpyxl sheet by its **cells**, not by its dimension tag — `classify_openpyxl` and `read_openpyxl` call
  `ws.reset_dimensions()` first — so an **understated** tag (v6.9 handled only an overstated one) no longer truncates the
  rows and columns read; `read_openpyxl` pads every row to the wider of the tag and the cells, so a builder's indexing is
  unchanged where the tag was right or overstated (every per-set output of the S-build byte-identical). Identifier wording
  now also covers numbered subjects — sample / patient / subject / individual / donor / case + a number ("Patient 9",
  "Subject_9", "Case 9") and "Ind" + a number ("Ind9"; case-sensitive: "IND1" is an HGNC alias) — and the bare headers
  "ID" and "Case" join the bare subject words; a number under a bare header always counts, and so does a short code —
  **v6.11 (C1-regression, post hoc, declared):** except under a bare "ID" header, and there only when EVERY short code below
  it is an HGNC approved / previous / alias symbol or an EXEMPT database id other than a year (a gene column headed "ID" is
  not a subject column; a column of text years under "ID" is). v6.10 had applied that exemption under every bare subject

  HGNC symbols or aliases) and let a bare "ID" over text years pass; each case is now a plant. The 20-row zone is

  verdict changed; one sheet no builder reads changed — SynGO 1.2's annotations.xlsx (Sheet1, clean → N; a member of the
  pinned zip whose genes.xlsx the S-build reads) — left whole (the zip is pinned by sha256). Re-run with v6.11's guard:
  no verdict changed (every sheet, builder-read or not).
